## DR-L2-ASPECT-1 — an aspect carries an elemental *relation*, not an element; the relation is computed as Δ mod 4

**Status:** VALIDATED · **Raised:** 2026-07-25 · **Validated:** 2026-07-25 · **By:** user (direct instruction) · **Domain:** L2' / M2-3 / M3'

**Context.** `TarotDecanService.elementForAspect` (landed 24.T24.7) shipped with an explicit `ARCHITECT-REVIEW` flag: it returns one element per aspect kind (`trine→Fire, square→Earth, opposition→Air, aspect→Water`) from an invented table, in the **legacy** m2.h `Element_Id` ordering. Two defects, one of them structural:

1. **Wrong ordering.** [[L2']] is THE element-bearing lens and its six inner positions are the one authoritative ordering (`0=Aether, 1=Earth, 2=Water, 3=Air, 4=Fire, 5=Salt`). `Body/S/S0/epi-lib/include/m_canonical.h` (05.T5.16) is the single point of truth for converting every legacy ordering to it, and the [[M2-3]] branch canon states plainly that runtime code "MUST use the helpers … never ad-hoc integer maps." The shipped function returns raw m2.h `Element_Id` values.
2. **Wrong shape.** No aspect→element assignment exists in canon because none can: an aspect is an angular relation between two zodiacal positions, so it carries an elemental *relation*, not an element. A five-element convention (Aether + the quartet) hides this by also dropping [[Salt]]; the true frame is the operative quartet held between the 0/5 poles.

**Decision (proposed).** Adopt the aspect law authored at [[L2']] §"Elemental Relation of Aspects":

- Sign→element is the triplicity identity `element_of(sign) = sign mod 4`, already carried by [[M2-3]]'s `decans[4][3][3][2]` first index (Fire = Aries·Leo·Sagittarius = `{0,4,8}`, etc.). The `#2-3-{1..4}` branch order *is* the zodiac's element cycle.
- An aspect spanning `Δ` signs fixes the elemental relation by `Δ mod 4`: `0` → same element (conjunction, trine); `2` → complementary pair, Fire↔Air / Earth↔Water (sextile, opposition); `3` → cross-pair (square).
- The relation ranges over the operative quartet only. [[Aether]] and [[Salt]] are never an aspect's element; they frame the wheel.

**Consequence.** `elementForAspect(aspect) → number` is unimplementable from canon — its return type asserts something an aspect does not carry — and must be replaced by a relation-shaped surface taking both positions. The shipped mapping is **not** canonical and must not be cited as precedent. Nothing in production consumes it yet (only its own unit test), so the surface change is free.

**Actions taken (2026-07-25).** Ratified by the Architect and landed:

- **`Body/M/pratibimba-app/src/engine/canonicalElement.ts`** — the TS counterpart of `m_canonical.h`: the six canonical element IDs (Salt included), `isOperativeElement`, the legacy conversions (`canonicalFromM2ElementId` / `m2ElementIdFromCanonical` / `canonicalFromM2_3Branch`, refusing with `CANONICAL_ELEMENT_INVALID` where an ordering has no counterpart — m2.h has no Salt), the triplicity identity (`signOfDegree` / `elementOfSign` / `elementOfDegree`), and the aspect law (`elementalRelationOfAspect`, `elementalRelationBetweenDegrees`).
- **`elementForAspect` WITHDRAWN** from `TarotDecanService`, along with its invented local `AspectKind = 'aspect' | …`. A tarot-decan service owned neither the element law nor the aspect vocabulary. The real five-member `AspectKind` already existed at `src/engine/clockFieldOverlay.ts`, which ports `m2_aspect_between` verbatim from the C kernel.
- **Wired where aspects actually live:** `AspectEdge` now carries `elements: { elementA, elementB, relation } | null`, computed from the bussed degrees. This is the consumption the 24.7 comment promised ("consumed by the cosmic-clock aspect-edge layer to colour chords by element") and never delivered — and it is now element-correct, since the elements come from the positions rather than from the aspect kind.
- **Proof:** 16 new tests in `canonicalElement.test.ts` + 1 wiring test in `clockFieldOverlay.test.ts`. The load-bearing one cross-checks `elementalRelationOfAspect(kind)` against `elementalRelationBetweenDegrees` at every exact major aspect around the whole circle, with the kernel-ported `aspectBetween` confirming each pair really is that aspect — one law, not two tables that happen to agree.

**Follow-on pass (2026-07-25): the unmarked scheme boundary.** A full audit of every element touchpoint in the carrier found **no live mis-keying** — both app-side tables are correct for their own inputs, because two element schemes are live BY DESIGN (DR-37-3 names five across the M-stack):

- **scheme A (m2.h tattva)** on the kernel-LUT-mirror path — `PLANET_ELEMENT_ID` (M2_PLANET_LUT) and `CHAKRA_ELEMENT_IDS` (M2_CHAKRA_LUT), consumed by `cosmicMath.ELEMENT_COLOURS`, `solarSystem.ELEMENT_NAMES`, `CymaticSpheresSurface`.
- **canonical-B (L2')** on everything crossing the M2↔M3 boundary, which the substrate normalises (`canonical_from_m2_tattva`, `canonical_from_m3_decan_element`) — e.g. `dominantElementId` on the medicine route, consumed by `MedicineViewPane`.

The defect was that **the boundary was unmarked**: a bare `elementId` gave no reader or compiler any way to tell which scheme it was in — the identical hazard that produced `elementForAspect`. Id `2` is Water canonically and Agni/Fire in the tattva ordering, so a mis-crossing renders a confidently wrong element rather than failing. Landed:

- **Every touchpoint now names its scheme** in-place (`ELEMENT_COLOURS`, `solarSystem.ELEMENT_NAMES`, `CymaticSpheresSurface` planet + chakra parsers, `MedicineViewPane`), each pointing at the converter needed to cross. `TATTVA_ELEMENT_NAMES` joins `canonicalElement.ts` so both schemes are named in one place; `solarSystem.ELEMENT_NAMES` now sources from it.
- **`TarotDecanChain.elementId` declared canonical-B.** The 24.7 landing called it "canonical m2.h `Element_Id`" — a contradiction in terms, since m2.h carries the tattva ordering. The chain crosses the M2↔M3 boundary, so canonical-B is the substrate law, not a choice.
- **The chain's element is now machine-checked against its sign.** The chain carries BOTH `zodiacSign` and `elementId`, so `elementId === elementOfSign(zodiacSign)` is checkable; a mismatch yields the honest-pending marker instead of a wrong element. This immediately caught the existing fixtures, which paired Capricorn (sign 9, Earth) with `elementId: 2` — a value matching *neither* scheme, which nothing had ever checked. Fixtures corrected.
- **`M3DecanChainBreadcrumb` renders the element NAME**, not the bare integer it was printing.
- **Rust doc lie fixed:** `portal-core/src/aspect.rs::element_name` was documented "Canonical lowercase element name" but returns scheme A, while `nara::medicine_frame::element_name` — same name, different module — is genuinely canonical-B. Same honesty correction DR-37-10 applied to the `canonical_from_medicine_rs_legacy` alias.

**Still deliberately unchanged.** `ELEMENT_COLOURS` and `solarSystem.ELEMENT_NAMES` remain keyed by scheme A. Re-keying them in place would silently repaint and relabel every live scene surface; the correct crossing is `m2ElementIdFromCanonical` at the call site, and a test now pins that (looking a canonical Fire up directly returns Prithvi/umber — the wrong colour).

---

## DR-L2-ELEM-2 — element registers are coordinate-owned; a common wire encoding is not ontological primacy

**Status:** VALIDATED · **Raised:** 2026-07-25 · **Validated:** 2026-07-25 · **By:** user (direct instruction) · **Domain:** M2-1 / M2-2 / M2-3 · **Supersedes the global-primacy reading in:** 05.T5.16, DR-37-3, [[L2']] §"Canonical Element IDs"

**The objection (Architect).** Why is there a *single* element canon at all, when the Bimba map already structures this precisely? [[L2']] is an MEF lens — and the MEF is [[M2-1]]. The medical body associations have their own specific relations and locations in the M2 branch. Treating one lens inside M2-1 as authority over its siblings inverts the structure.

**Confirmed by the data.** `m2.c` labels the tattva block outright — `/* Mahabhutas — the 5 elements (throughline anchor) */`, tattvas 31–35. So the ordering called "m2.h `Element_Id`", "medicine.rs legacy", or "scheme A" across the stack **is [[M2-2]]'s Mahābhūta series** (`tattva_index − 31`), a coordinate-owned register with its own principle (Śaiva emanation, space densifying to earth) — not a legacy accident. And [[M2-1]] is the MEF itself (`mef_lenses[12][6]`), so L2' is one lens-family inside it. Two *distinct body ontologies* were also being fused as "medicine": the chakra↔tattva yogic body (M2-2) and the decan↔body-part Hermetic medical-astrology body (M2-3).

**Decision.**
1. **There is no "the" element id.** Each register belongs to its coordinate and is complete there: [[M2-2]] Mahābhūta, [[M2-1]]/L2' alchemical, [[M2-3]] triplicity, plus m3.h's clock register and m4.h's nucleotide order. "Legacy" is retired as a description of any of them.
2. **Mappings between registers are correspondences — claims with content — not casts.** Ākāśa ↔ Aether asserts something. Salt has no Mahābhūta counterpart because Salt is a Tria Prima principle and not a mahābhūta; the series is not missing a member, it is a different system. Partiality is the correct behaviour, not a shortfall.
3. **A common wire encoding is retained and is not a primacy claim.** The M-stack keeps serialising in the alchemical register when an element crosses a coordinate boundary, so a receiver can know which register it is reading. That is a serialisation choice. The `Canonical_*` symbols are historical names for it.
4. **A lens confers a reading, not ownership.** L2' remains THE element-bearing lens — the aperture for reading any coordinate alchemically. It does not own M2-2's mahābhūtas or M2-3's triplicities.

**Landed.**
- `pratibimba-app/src/engine/canonicalElement.ts` → **`elementRegisters.ts`**, with every export named by owning register: `AlchemicalElement`/`ALCHEMICAL_ELEMENT_NAMES`, `MAHABHUTA_NAMES`/`MAHABHUTA_TATTVA_BASE`, `alchemicalFromMahabhuta`/`mahabhutaFromAlchemical` (documented as correspondences), `alchemicalFromTriplicityBranch`, `triplicityOfSign`/`triplicityOfDegree`, `ELEMENT_REGISTER_INVALID`.
- Every carrier touchpoint renamed from "scheme A / canonical-B / legacy" to its owning coordinate ([[M2-2]] Mahābhūta register / alchemical register), including the user-visible `M3TranscriptionEngine` label that read `canonical-B:`.
- `m_canonical.h` and `nara/medicine_frame.rs` reframed: registers named by coordinate, correspondences described as claims, wire encoding distinguished from primacy. Symbols unchanged (no churn); their historical names are annotated.
- `[[L2']]` canon amended — the "one authoritative element ordering for the entire Epi-Logos system" claim withdrawn in place, with a sibling-registers table and the lens-confers-a-reading distinction.

**Verification.** carrier 1337 unit tests pass / typecheck 0 / build 0; `make test` 60/60 on `m_canonical`; `cargo check` clean for `portal-core` and `epi-cli`.

**Follow-through (2026-07-25, same day) — all three items closed.**

1. **A distinct type per register.** `Mahabhuta`, `Alchemical` and `TriplicityBranch` are branded types; the only ways in are the validating constructors (`asMahabhuta` / `asAlchemical` / `asTriplicityBranch`) and the documented correspondences. `ELEMENT_COLOURS` is keyed by `Mahabhuta`, so looking up an alchemical id **no longer compiles**. Every crossing in the carrier is now explicit and validated where the bus value enters. The refusals are themselves tested with `@ts-expect-error`, so `tsc` fails if any of them ever starts type-checking. `triplicityOfSign` now answers in two steps — M2-3's own branch coordinate (`#2-3-N = 1 + sign mod 4`) and then the correspondence onto the wire — so M2-3 keeps its own numbering instead of borrowing M2-1's.
2. **Correspondences are canon; code is the mirror.** `Idea/Bimba/Map/datasets/m2-element-registers.json` declares every register (owning coordinate, ordering principle, members, provenance) and every correspondence (tradition, the claim it makes, its partiality and why). `elementRegisters.canon.test.ts` proves the runtime agrees member-for-member and pair-for-pair, so the declaration is the source and drift fails the build — verified by injecting a wrong pairing and watching it go red.
3. **The two body ontologies are named apart.** The medicine surface had fused M2-2's yogic body (chakra → tattva → mahābhūta → zone) with M2-3's Hermetic medical body (decan → sign → body part → herb) into one undifferentiated "medicine". Each panel now carries `data-body-ontology` and its owning coordinate, and `activeChakraId` — the one place the two meet, reached decan → element → chakra via `chakra_for_element` — renders as a **correspondence between two body ontologies**, not a fact of one body.

**Verification:** typecheck 0, build 0, carrier-tokens GREEN, honesty-lint clean, `make test` all suites passed, 1362 carrier tests pass.

## DR-S2-ALIAS-1 — `property_mapping.rs` is the canonical-name authority; measured-identical alias keys retire to it

**Status:** VALIDATED · **Raised:** 2026-07-25 · **Validated:** 2026-07-25 · **By:** user (direct instruction — "retire the migration-artefact aliases in favour of the better names") · **Domain:** S2 graph data · **Record:** [[graph-data-normalisation]]

**The problem.** The Bimba corpus carried the same semantic under multiple `{family}_{n}_` keys — `c_1_context_frame` beside `c_3_context_frame`, `t_3_last_updated` beside `c_3_updated_at`, `key_principles` spread across five position digits. Read-time tolerance (`ROLE_RULES` in `property-roles.ts`, the Open-Schema Doctrine) papered over it, so nothing forced a resolution.

**Decision.**
1. **The canonical name is the one `dataset_import/property_mapping.rs` maps to.** That table (115 targets) is the authority; it is what the importer would write today. Not the higher-volume key, not the lower position digit.
2. **An alias retires only when measured 100% identical**, with zero conflicting and zero canonical-absent rows. Name similarity is a candidate, never a warrant.
3. **The candidate set is derived, not enumerated.** Group every prefixed key by semantic suffix; any suffix served by ≥2 keys is a candidate pair. This found 40 real pairs where five had been reported.
4. **The write re-asserts identity in its own predicate**, so it cannot drop a non-duplicate even if the measurement went stale between measuring and writing.

**Landed.** 40 alias keys retired, 2,955 property instances, zero code readers and zero vault producers among them. Two candidates **excluded** after measurement proved them distinct properties, not aliases: `t_3_updated_at` (378 of 397 co-present rows hold a *different* timestamp) and `s_4_description` (16 conflicting, 6 canonical-absent). Also landed: `c_4_subsystem` cast to its majority INTEGER (39 nodes — 38 numeric strings and one `["4","4"]` double-write), leaving that key single-typed.

**Verification.** Dry run reproduced the measurements exactly before writing. After: all 40 aliases at 0 remaining; every canonical twin's count unchanged; both excluded keys untouched; `:Bimba` node count 2,098 and edge count 11,295 unchanged. Verified through a different client than the one that wrote it. Reversal snapshots committed at `Seeds/S/S2/normalisation-snapshots/`.

## DR-S2-LAYER-1 — `c_4_layer` is two semantics sharing one key; the data stays mixed and readers tolerate both

**Status:** VALIDATED · **Raised:** 2026-07-25 · **Validated:** 2026-07-25 · **By:** user (chose "leave the 84 alone") · **Domain:** S2 graph data / `graph_api.rs` · **Record:** [[graph-data-normalisation]]

**The finding.** `c_4_layer` was reported as a type collision to be normalised to its STRING majority. It is not a collision. STRING (1,956) is a **controlled node-kind vocabulary** — `COORDINATE`, `CONTEXT_FRAME`, `PSYCHOID`, `FAMILY_META`, `LENS`, `VAK`, `WEAVE`, `FAMILY_ROOT` — exactly what `seed.rs::merge_node(layer: &str)` writes. INTEGER (84) is the **S-stack layer index** `0`–`5`, 14 nodes per layer, every one `:Coordinate:Stack` and stamped `sync_version: m5-s-lattice-migration-…`. Casting `0` → `"0"` would have written a non-vocabulary value into a closed column.

**Decision.** Leave the 84 as they are. The integer is a second semantic, not a bad cast, and no new key is invented to hold it (a `c_4_layer` value and an S-layer index are different questions). **Readers tolerate both types.**

**Landed.** `bimba_node_row` read `row.get::<String>("layer").unwrap_or_default()`, which returned `Err` for all 84 and silently yielded `""` — so **every S-stack coordinate served an empty layer** through `s2.graph.node`. This is a second instance of the silent-empty-string failure mode that `a8cb2862` fixed on the RETURN projector. Repaired with `string_or_int_field`, which renders the integer as its decimal string so the JSON wire type stays `string` and no consumer shape changes.

**Verification.** `tests/mixed_type_layer_projection_live.rs` pins both branches against the real corpus — `S3` → `"3"`, `M3` → `"COORDINATE"` — and was confirmed to fail before the fix (`left: ""`, `right: "3"`), so it is not vacuous. graph-services 253 tests pass.

**Recorded, not patched.** `seed.rs::seed_node_group_counts` counts `c_4_layer = 'COORDINATE'`; the 12 S-family roots are seeded with that string but were later overwritten to INTEGER by the lattice migration, so `family_coordinates` reads 12 low. It is a diagnostic snapshot, not an assertion. Teaching the seeder that "INTEGER layer also means COORDINATE" would encode the drift as semantics, which is a canon question and is left open.

## DR-S2-TIME-1 — `c_3_updated_at` normalises to its producer's type, not to its majority

**Status:** VALIDATED · **Raised:** 2026-07-25 · **Validated:** 2026-07-25 · **By:** user (chose "to ZONED DATETIME — follow the writer") · **Domain:** S2 graph data · **Record:** [[graph-data-normalisation]] · **Amends for this key:** [[DR-S2-ALIAS-1]]'s "collision keys to majority" rule

**The finding.** `c_3_updated_at` split STRING 1,108 / ZONED DATETIME 6, so "to majority" meant STRING. But the **only live writer** is `sync/coordinator.rs:350` — `SET … n.c_3_updated_at = datetime()` — which emits ZONED DATETIME. The 1,108 strings are the legacy dataset-import corpus (`property_mapping.rs:330`). Normalising to the majority would have obeyed the instruction and re-split the column on the very next vault sync: a treadmill, not a normalisation.

**Decision.** Normalise **toward the producer**, i.e. to the minority type. Where a column's majority disagrees with its only writer, the writer wins — otherwise the data is corrected against the thing that keeps re-creating it.

**Landed.** 1,108 STRING → ZONED DATETIME, lossless. All 1,108 matched strict ISO-8601 with an explicit `Z`; the pre-flight gate required 1108/1108 seconds **and** nanoseconds preserved or it refused to write; post-verify read back out of the graph confirmed 1108/1108 equal to `datetime(original)`. The only change is that redundant zero-padding stops being stored (`.979000000Z` → `.979Z`, identical `epochMillis` and `nanosecond`) — the strings had been carrying **fake** nanosecond precision, since three were second-precision and one millisecond-precision, all padded to nine digits.

**Result.** `c_3_updated_at` is single-typed `ZONED DATETIME × 1114` and matches its producer, so it stays that way.

**Follow-through 2026-07-26 — `t_3_updated_at` retired, and one finding retracted.**

- **`t_3_updated_at` removed** (397 instances) on the Architect's call ("redundant, get rid"). It was never an alias — 378 of its 397 co-present rows held a *different* timestamp, which is why [[DR-S2-ALIAS-1]] excluded it from the alias pass. Removed as redundant instead, with values snapshotted (they were not recoverable from a twin). Every one of the 397 carried `c_3_updated_at`, so no node lost its timestamp. `ROLE_RULES.updated_at` in `property-roles.ts` no longer lists it: that entry was a real mis-declaration, since serving a key that disagrees with the canonical on 95% of rows answers with the wrong time. `t_3_last_updated` stays in the chain — 1101/1101 identical, a true alias.
- **The seed-counter drift spot is closed, not recorded.** `seed_node_group_counts` now admits both `c_4_layer` representations, so the 12 S-family roots stop reading as seed loss on a fresh-graph comparison. Exact rather than widening: an INTEGER `c_4_layer` occurs only on `:Coordinate:Stack` nodes (`integer_but_not_stack` = 0).
- **RETRACTED — the "m-family key drift" finding was a false positive.** `property_mapping.rs`'s 3-part m-entries are templates, not stored names: `canonicalize_prime_surface_property` rebuilds each key with the prefix computed from the node's own coordinate (`#3-5` → `m_3_5`), pinned by `m_prime_properties_use_coordinate_slot_prefixes` (`#3-5-8` + `degree: 248` → `n.m_3_5_degree = 248`). M3-5 is the correct, more precise coordinate mapping and the world clock's full 360°/24h structure is honoured. The corpus shows the 4-part form is required, not merely tolerated: `amino_acid_code` is owned by M3-2, M3-3 AND M3-4, so flattening to `m_3_amino_acid_code` would collapse three coordinate-owned registers into one — the category error [[DR-L2-ELEM-2]] names. No change made; none was needed.

## DR-M1-FACE-LAYOUT-1 — `standalone-ide-deep` is BOTH-gated: the deep layout opens it, the face selects it

**Status:** VALIDATED · **Raised:** 2026-07-29 · **Validated:** 2026-07-29 · **By:** tranche [[52.T2]] (the tranche requires the choice be recorded, not left implied by a conditional) · **Domain:** M' carrier surface law / `Body/M/pratibimba-app/src/panes/m1SurfaceDispatch.tsx` · **Amends:** [[DR-WC-M1-1]] (the mode inventory is unchanged; only its gate is)

**The finding.** `resolveM1SurfaceContext` took `{ face, cosmicComposition? }` and never received the shell's real `activeLayout`. It then *manufactured* one: `face === 1 ⇒ { mode: 'standalone-ide-deep', layoutId: 'ide-deep' }`, everything else ⇒ `layoutId: 'daily-0-1'`. So "the user is on the personal face" MEANT "the user is in the 4+2 depth layout". The shell has never believed this — `App.tsx` carries `face` and `activeLayout` as independent state — and `DCC-07` ([[M5'-SPEC]] :107, :161) holds shell `1` and full `4+2` depth as distinct authority classes that "must not be treated as interchangeable artifact or authority classes". At boot the carrier sits on face 1 in `daily-0-1`, so the *default* state of the app was the collapsed one.

**The decision — BOTH-gated, not layout-alone and not face-alone.** The standalone deep page requires `face === 1` AND `activeLayout === 'ide-deep'`. The full four-cell resolution:

| face | layout | mode | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `daily-0-1` | `composed-cosmic-1-2-3` | the cosmic 1-2-3 composition contribution |
| 0 | `ide-deep` | `composed-cosmic-1-2-3` | still the composition; only `layoutId` changes |
| 1 | `daily-0-1` | `compact-track-08` | shell `1` **previews**; depth is withheld |
| 1 | `ide-deep` | `standalone-ide-deep` | both gates open |

- **The LAYOUT gate is canon.** The eight-slot M1' workbench is depth, and [[M5'-SPEC]] :161 is explicit: depth "belongs to the deep 4+2 subsystem layer. It should not be compressed into the daily shell. Shell `1` may PREVIEW". `compact-track-08` — the composed body minus the cosmic cross-pole — is exactly that preview, and it already existed; it had simply never been reachable in production because only tests passed `cosmicComposition: false`.
- **The FACE gate is role, not depth.** Face 0's mount is the 1-2-3 *composition* contribution — a composition role. Entering the deep layout must not turn the cosmic composition tab into the M1' workbench, so the deep mode stays off face 0. Rejected alternative: layout-gated alone, which would have put the workbench on both faces in `ide-deep`.
- **`layoutId` is now reported on every branch, never derived.** This is the half of the repair that has no policy content: the field must equal the shell's `activeLayout` whatever the mode. It was wrong in two of the four cells.

**Landed.** `resolveM1SurfaceContext` takes a required `activeLayout: LayoutId` (the [[52.T1]] authority type — imported, never re-declared). `App.tsx`'s two mount sites hand it `parseLayoutId(activeLayout)`; a context-less `M1SurfaceDispatchPane` falls back to `DEFAULT_LAYOUT_ID`, the daily ground state, never to depth. The three bodies expose `data-layout-id` so the axis is observable in the running shell. `CHROME-CONTRACT.md`'s `m1SurfaceComposed` / `m1SurfaceDeep` rows were corrected in the same change — the `m1SurfaceDeep` row had asserted `standalone-ide-deep` unconditionally.

**Verification.** Unit tests cover all four `(face, layout)` cells plus the standalone-uniqueness sweep; five of them were proven RED against the restored pre-52.T2 body before the fix was reinstated. A UF e2e (`tests/e2e/m1-surface-face-layout.spec.ts`) drives real Chromium against the real spawned gateway: at boot (face 1, `daily-0-1`) the M1 mount renders the preview with `data-layout-id="daily-0-1"` and the deep slots absent — the exact case the old code got wrong; entering `ide-deep` through the existing cross-layout intent brings the workbench up with the face unmoved; and face 0 in `ide-deep` still renders the composition while reporting the real layout.

**Recorded, not patched.** (a) The face-1 residency of the M1 deep mount is a *carrier* fact, not a canon claim — [[M5'-SPEC]] assigns M1'-depth to the structural half. [[52.T4]]/[[52.T5]] decide whether the deep layout is per-face at all, and this row's FACE gate is the surface to revisit then, not a law to preserve. (b) The tab is still named "M1 Deep" while rendering a preview in `daily-0-1`; renaming bumps `LAYOUT_VERSION` and invalidates saved layouts, so it is left to the tranche that touches the models. The body's `data-m1-surface-mode` tells the truth in the meantime.

## DR-DEEP-LAYOUT-1 — the deep layout is PER-FACE: four (face × layout) models, not one deep workspace

**Status:** VALIDATED · **Raised:** 2026-07-29 · **Validated:** 2026-07-29 · **By:** tranche [[52.T4]] (the tranche requires the choice be recorded, not left implied by a signature) · **Domain:** M' carrier layout law / `Body/M/pratibimba-app/src/App.tsx` + `src/ui/deepPaneSet.ts` · **Answers:** [[DR-M1-FACE-LAYOUT-1]]'s deferred question — "[[52.T4]]/[[52.T5]] decide whether the deep layout is per-face at all"

**The question.** `ideDeepDefault()` cannot have a signature until this is answered: is the deep layout **per-face** (a deep model for each of the two faces) or **face-independent** (one deep workspace the face axis does not reach)? [[52.T4]] names both pressures — [[M5'-SPEC]] :159's one-shell language ("the omni panel switches layouts inside one process; there is no second app") and the orthogonality of face and layout that [[52.T2]] had just restored — and observes that the answer changes `factory(node, activeLayout)`'s shape. It does more than that: it changes the shell's render tree.

**Decision: PER-FACE.** `ideDeepDefault(model: DeepPaneModelId, activeLayout)` takes the face, so the shell holds four flexlayout models — one per (face, layout) cell — and `modelOf(models, face, layout)` is a two-axis LOOKUP, never a derivation from either axis alone.

| | `daily-0-1` | `ide-deep` |
|---|---|---|
| face 0 (cosmic) | `cosmicDefault` → `cosmic-main` (13 tabs) | `ideDeepDefault('cosmic')` → `cosmic-deep-main` (12 tabs) |
| face 1 (personal) | `personalDefault` → `personal-main` (10 tabs) | `ideDeepDefault('personal')` → `personal-deep-main` (8 tabs) |

Four reasons, in order of weight:

- **The depth material is already face-conjugate in canon.** [[M5'-SPEC]] :161 does not describe one undifferentiated depth: "The 4+2 subsystem pages deliver the depth: M0' through M3' for structural/instrument work, M4' for Nara, and M5' for the Epii IDE." That is the same structural-vs-lived cut the two faces already are. A face-independent deep layout would have had to erase a distinction canon draws *inside* the deep layer itself.
- **Face-independence collapses an axis — the DCC-07 error [[52.T2]] just repaired, running the other way.** With one deep model, `face.toggle` becomes a no-op (or a silent layout exit) for as long as the user stays in depth. [[52.T2]] removed exactly this class of collapse from `resolveM1SurfaceContext`, where face was *manufacturing* a layout; letting layout suppress face would trade one collapsed axis for another. DCC-07 ([[M5'-SPEC]] :107) holds the two as distinct authority classes in both directions, not just the one that had been violated.
- **"One shell" is a process claim, not a model claim — and per-face is what KEEPS the shell one.** The daily layout has always been two models inside one shell; two more change nothing structural. The shell still renders exactly two `<Layout>` slots (`face-active` / `face-hidden`), the switch is still the OmniPanel's, the four stores are still singletons, the `/` membrane is still shared. A face-independent deep layout needs a THIRD render branch that hides both face slots — a shell whose *shape* depends on the layout, which is nearer to "a second app" than the thing the one-shell sentence forbids.
- **A single deep tabset would have made the flat-tabset defect worse, not better.** Track 52's own brief names it: "M0–M5 depth is scattered as ~24 sibling tabs inside the two face tabsets." One deep model means ~20 depth surfaces in ONE strip, and the carrier already has a measured finding that an 11th `personal-main` tab makes the strip un-clickable at 1280×800. Per-face splits them 12/8. The real answer to depth organisation is [[52.T5]]'s six subsystem pages, which nest INSIDE these two deep models; the face axis is the coarse M0'–M3' / M4'–M5' partition :161 already draws, not a substitute for them.

**Rejected alternatives.** (a) *One face-independent deep model* — rejected for the four reasons above. (b) *Per-face by reusing the DAILY models with a layout-conditioned factory* — one pane set whose bodies render differently in depth. Rejected because that is what the carrier already did (`bimbaGraph` and `m1SurfaceDeep` are depth-differentiated bodies) and it is precisely why `ide-deep` "selected no pane set": a layout that changes only what panes CONTAIN, never which panes EXIST, is a rendering mode, not a layout. (c) *Deep as a per-face modifier that adds/removes tabs in the daily model in place* — rejected because one persisted layout tree cannot honestly hold two pane sets; a saved layout would restore whichever set was last serialised into whichever layout the shell resumed into.

**Landed.** `ideDeepDefault(model, activeLayout)` in `App.tsx`, and the composition itself is a DECLARATION in `src/ui/deepPaneSet.ts` — `DEEP_PANE_SET` (22 mounts), `DEEP_PANE_WITHDRAWALS` (8), `DEEP_PANE_RESERVATIONS` (3) — rather than layout JSON buried in the shell, so it can be held against [[CHROME-CONTRACT]] §2 in both directions by `src/ui/deepPaneSet.test.ts`. `LAYOUT_VERSION` 23 → 24 and `PersistedUiState` gained `personalDeep` / `cosmicDeep`, so a layout switch does not discard the tab the user left open in the other layout. Each cell's model is built with ITS OWN layout id, so the `/` membrane's per-layout fold filter belongs to the model it is part of instead of to whichever layout the shell happened to boot into. The two `<Layout>` keys carry `activeLayout`, which keeps [[52.T3]]'s `routingRevision` remount honest for the surfaces that exist in both cells. `src/ui/shellSlotPolicy.ts` gained `layoutVaried` and was reconciled against the shell as built: `left` and `main` are what actually differ per layout, while `bottom` — previously the ONLY slot declared per-layout — is composed by neither carrier layout, so its row now states the law for an uninhabited slot rather than implying a per-layout shell that does not exist.

**The negative half is load-bearing.** The three designated `pending` surfaces canon places in depth — `agenticControlRoom` ([[28.T28.5]]), `coordinateTree` (28.T28.6), `backendStudio` (28.T28.13) — are RESERVED with their decided slot and model and deliberately NOT mounted: a `pending` id found in the factory fails `chromeContract.test.ts`, and mounting one would steal its tranche's deliverable. So `ideDeepDefault()` lands the layout without pre-empting the surfaces that belong in it, and each of those tranches inherits a position rather than re-deciding the layout.

**Verification.** 25 unit assertions across `src/ui/deepPaneSet.test.ts` (16) + `src/ui/shellSlotPolicy.test.tsx` (9) hold the declaration against [[CHROME-CONTRACT]] §2 in both directions, against an AST walk of the real `App.tsx` registry, and against `CROSS_LAYOUT_INTENT_TARGETS` (a receiver an intent can carry into depth must be mounted there). A UF e2e (`tests/e2e/deep-pane-set.spec.ts`) drives real Chromium against the real spawned gateway: it enters depth through [[52.T3]]'s OmniPanel control, reads the live pane set off the shell's `data-layout-pane-set` and asserts it equals the declared deep set on both faces — every mount present, every withdrawal absent, every reservation absent — renders a real deep body on each face (`standalone-ide-deep` on personal, full-lattice `bimbaGraph` on cosmic), re-applies the §2 status law to what the browser actually mounted, and returns through the same control to a daily pane set byte-identical to the one it left, with the seven-field cross-layout identity receipt intact in both directions.

**Recorded, not patched.** (a) [[DR-M1-FACE-LAYOUT-1]]'s FACE gate on `m1SurfaceDeep` STANDS, and is now reachable in production for the first time: face 1 + `ide-deep` is a real cell, so `standalone-ide-deep` opens when both gates open. That row's "revisit at 52.T4" note is discharged by this decision, not overturned. (b) `semanticConnections` sits in BOTH the daily face-1 rail and the deep explorer rail while `LEFT_SIDEBAR_MODES` declares `smart-connections` as `availableInLayouts: ['ide-deep']`. [[52.T6]] owns settling that contradiction; 52.T4 records only that the deep rail is where the deep-only declaration finally has a home, and changes neither declaration. (c) The deep left rail is hardcoded FlexLayout children exactly as the daily one is — making the slot genuinely activity-bar-switched is [[52.T6]], which is why `shellSlotPolicy`'s `left` row keeps `policy: 'activity-bar-switched'` while gaining `layoutVaried: true`. It OPENS CLOSED (flexlayout's own `selected: -1`), which is the opening-tab law of (e) applied to the left slot — `fileTree` would otherwise mount on BOTH faces the instant the layout is entered, two unprompted vault reads for a surface nobody asked for — and is also the honest pre-[[52.T6]] state of an activity-bar-switched slot: until the mode registry runs, no mode IS the current one. (d) `pratibimbaCoordinate` is withdrawn from depth despite :161 naming the integrated 4/5/0 path as deep material, because [[52.T3]]'s landed cross-layout audit gives that receiver `preferredLayout: 'daily-0-1'` and mounting it in depth would contradict the ledger; [[52.T5]]'s M4' subsystem page is where the recognition path gets its deep home. (e) **THE OPENING-TAB LAW, and the one thing this tranche chose to order around rather than fix.** Both faces' deep models render on layout entry and FlexLayout's `tabEnableRenderOnDemand` (default true) mounts the SELECTED tab of each tabset — so each model's first declared mount comes up with no gesture asking for it, on the hidden face too. 52.T4 first opened the personal deep tabset on `m1SurfaceDeep`, which embeds `WalkPane` (`m1-slot-walk`), whose mount effect auto-arrives at seed `'M1'` and publishes it into the ONE shared coordinate store — so merely entering depth silently re-pointed every M0'/M1' subscriber and broke `tests/e2e/error-ux-grammar.spec.ts:160` (32.T32.7), which needs the M0 language reader genuinely empty. `canonUpdateLedger` opens instead (:161 names M5' the Epii IDE, so governance is the right opening for the lived/governance half), and the law is now machine-enforced: `DeepPaneMount.mountPublishes` declares the hazard anchored to the real source, and `deepPaneSet.test.ts` fails closed both if such a mount is ever ordered first AND if the anchor stops matching — so the flag cannot outlive the behaviour. The underlying defect is WalkPane's, not the layout's: a pane writing a shared singleton from an unprompted mount effect is a state seizure whichever layout hosts it. It is NOT repaired here — that is a 22.T22.1 / T2.5 surface with its own specs, and 52.T4 declines to change another tranche's publication semantics on the way past. Flagged for the M1'-walk owner.

## DR-ACR-1 — the ACR's two spec-named methods do not exist: `review.transition` is corrected to `review.resolve`, `epii.runtime_control` is declared unwired and rendered as such

**Status:** VALIDATED · **Raised:** 2026-07-27 (recon) · **Validated:** 2026-07-30 · **By:** tranche [[28.T28.5]] · **Domain:** M5' governance chrome / `Body/M/pratibimba-app/src/panes/acr/*` · **Class:** the same class as [[28.T28.4]]'s `s1'.semantic.suggest` correction — a Wave-C spec naming a gateway method the substrate never registered

**The question.** 28.5 (a) specifies five T8 contents, two of them bound to named gateway methods: `<ReviewDecisionControls>` "wraps `s5'.review.transition`" and `<AbortRetryContinueControls>` whose "abort/retry/continue commands [are] wrapped via `s5'.epii.runtime_control`". Neither method is registered anywhere in `Body/S`. Both appear only in the FROZEN `Body/M/epi-theia/extensions/ide-shell-m0-m5/src/browser/acr/*`, which is dead plumbing whose CONTRACT surfaces are law and whose wiring is not. A tranche can build against a name that does not exist in exactly three ways — invent a client for a method the gateway will refuse, silently retarget onto a sibling and say nothing, or record the correction — and only the third survives review.

**Decision, in two halves, because the two methods fail differently.**

**(1) `s5'.review.transition` → `s5'.review.resolve`.** The S5 review surface is four methods, declared in one table: `Body/S/S5/epii-review-core/src/s5_handlers.rs::S5_REVIEW_METHODS` = `submit` / `inbox` / `resolve` / `history`. `submit` OPENS a `ReviewInboxItem`; `resolve` is the transition on an open one, and it is the arm that carries the human gate (`requires_human_resolution(item, decision)` refuses a non-`Human` `resolved_by`). So the spec's "transition" is `resolve`, reached from `inbox`, and the decision control rides both. This is a **retarget, not a downgrade**: the capability the spec asked for exists in full, under a different name, with the gate the spec wanted already enforced substrate-side.

**(2) `s5'.epii.runtime_control` → UNWIRED.** The `s5'.epii.*` family is owned by `Body/S/S5/epii-agent-core/src/s5_handlers/mod.rs` and its arms are `deposit`, `deposit.list`, `deposit.typed_candidate`, `axiom_translate`, `axiom_translation_history`, `status`, `runtime.context`, `user.orientation`, `pratibimba.status`, `workbench.*`, `kairos.context`. There is no run-lifecycle arm, and no other coordinate registers one: **a dispatch cannot be aborted, retried, or continued from any surface in this carrier.** This is a real capability gap, not a naming one. `<AbortRetryContinueControls>` therefore renders as an **honest pending-wire surface** — the three affordances present and permanently disabled, beside the method name and the reason — taking the [[32.T32.7]] precedent, which recorded one of four error paths as `unwired` with its reason rather than deep-linking to an artefact nothing produces. The spec's `humanRequired` gate is rendered too, separately, because it is the half that is real: even once a method lands, a human-gated run must not be abortable here.

**Rejected alternatives.** (a) *Build a client for `s5'.review.transition` and let the gateway refuse it* — an unregistered method returns a wire error, so the control would be a button whose only behaviour is to fail; and the failure would read as a substrate outage rather than as a spec-vs-substrate mismatch. (b) *Wire abort/retry/continue onto `s4'.mediation.route`* — route DISPATCHES; it has no lifecycle verbs, and dressing three buttons in a method that cannot honour them is the fake control this register exists to prevent. (c) *Drop the controls entirely* — then the gap is invisible and the next reader re-derives it; the disabled surface with its reason is the artefact that makes the missing method findable. (d) *Record the corrections only in a code comment* — a comment is not a decision; the surface itself now carries the seam register (`acrGovernance.ts::ACR_METHOD_BINDINGS`, rendered as the pane's "Substrate seams" section) so a reader of the running app meets the correction, not just a reader of the source.

**The register is bidirectional and machine-held.** `ACR_METHOD_BINDINGS` carries every seam the surface rides with `status: 'live' | 'unwired'`, and two laws are asserted: an `unwired` seam MUST state a reason and a `live` one MUST NOT excuse itself (the `ui/errorUxGrammar.ts` discipline), and a `correction` exists exactly when `specNamed` does. Crucially the CLAIM ITSELF is tested against the substrate rather than against the comment: `acrGovernance.test.ts` runs `git grep` over `Body/S/**/*.rs` and fails if `s5'.review.transition` or `s5'.epii.runtime_control` ever appears there, or if `inbox` / `resolve` / `submit` / `deposit` ever stops appearing. If the missing method lands, this DR's second half goes red on the next run instead of quietly outliving its truth.

**Landed.** `src/panes/acr/` — `acrGovernance.ts` (the seam register, the IOD-17 parity computation, the DR-M5-1 roster projection), `acrReviewInbox.ts` (strict `ReviewInbox` projection + the `ReviewResolveRequest` body), `PiRuntimeMonitorBanner.tsx`, `Iod17ParityMatrix.tsx`, `ReviewDecisionControls.tsx`, `AbortRetryContinueControls.tsx`, `AgenticControlRoomPane.tsx`. The pane is mounted DEEP-ONLY in `personal-deep-main` ([[DR-DEEP-LAYOUT-1]]'s reserved position, consumed): [[CHROME-CONTRACT]] §2 `agenticControlRoom` flips `pending` → `live`, `App.tsx::factory` gains its case, and the entry moves from `DEEP_PANE_RESERVATIONS` into `DEEP_PANE_SET` — deliberately not first, per the opening-tab law, because the pane reads three gateway seams on mount. It declares no `mountPublishes`: its mount effects are gateway READS only, and the sole shared-store write it can cause is the OmniPanel routing a user's evidence click.

**Recorded, not patched.** (a) The T8 contents 28.5 (a) names are NOT rebuilt: [[27.T27.10]] already migrated RunTree → `DispatchGenealogyTree`, ToolStream → `ToolStreamPanel`, EvidenceDepositForm → `panes/omni/evidence/EvidenceDepositForm.tsx` over the live `s5'.epii.deposit`. The ACR composes them in their deep governance reading; a second form over the same method would be two writers of one contract. The ToolStream is the one exception to "composes" and is deliberate: [[DR-WC-IS-2]] makes the time-ordered render AGENTIC primary, so instead of a second `ToolStreamPanel` (two readers of one dataset, two copies of one tab state) the ACR fires the canonical 27.9 route `ide-shell-m0-m5/agentic-control-room.select-run` — the spec's OWN click-through for this surface — carrying the selected node id into the `/` membrane. Structural fold here, temporal fold there, one node identity across both (15.11). 28.5 (e)'s Aletheia subagent trace is likewise already carried inside `DispatchGenealogyTree` (`AletheiaCrystallisationGroup` + the non-blocking `VetoBanner`, 12.19). (b) 28.5 (b)'s roster collapse was ALREADY LANDED by 27.T27.10 in `omnipanelCapabilities.ts`; this tranche projects it (`acrRoster`) rather than re-enumerating it, so a guardian added there appears here and Sophia cannot re-acquire an actor row by being retyped in a second place. (c) The `ide-shell-m0-m5/agentic-control-room` cross-layout target still resolves to `omniDispatchTrace` with `preferredLayout: null`, and is deliberately NOT retargeted: [[DR-WC-IS-2]] gives the always-on abbreviated render to the `/` membrane, so a capacity-lane click-through belongs there and not in a layout the user may not be in. (d) `backendStudio` click-through from a RunTree node (28.5 (d)) is HELD rather than routed — that surface is §2 `pending` and 28.T28.13 owns it; the source anchor is disclosed with its reason instead of deep-linking to nothing, which is the same discipline as (2) above. (e) The IOD-17 third face is this surface's own standing declaration — "the ACR never enables an agent committal" — and is deliberately NOT derived from the other two, or the three-cell matrix would be one cell asserted three times.
